### 2026-10-28 — RC5 Crafty1 no-holder burst follow-up

- The newly referenced upload was not present at `C:\Users\elibo\Downloads\latest.log`, `1latest.log`, or `2latest.log` during this review. No fresh hash is claimed. The preserved earlier Crafty1 bytes remain identified in `performance-results/rc5-crafty1-moonrise-fallback-log-analysis-20261028.md` (SHA-256 `920B665985CB2066D2199F0A6795C438D268FEADE91E8BE1246EAEA00CCA25D1`); they record 273 no-holder warnings at 20:09:52–20:09:53, but contain no chest coordinates or chest-specific diagnosis.
- Source trace confirmed the warning is generated by Moonrise/Paper's region read when there is no local `NewChunkHolder`. `MultiPaper.readRegionFileAsync` routes that case to `ForceReadChunkMessage`; Master `ForceReadChunkHandler` reads Master-owned `region` storage through `RegionFileCache`, after `ChunkLockManager.waitForLock`. This is a designed Master-backed fallback, not an instruction to read the backend's local region file. The warning text “straight from disk” is ambiguous. The path can only return stale content if the Master snapshot was already stale or write/ownership ordering was wrong; the warning does not establish either corruption or freshness.
- Reused the hash-pinned isolated Java 25 result in `performance-results/rc5-crafty1-273-noholder-read-stress-20261028.md`: 273 production-path fallback reads, bounded to 32 workers, all returned exact seeded single-chest diamond x32 and double-chest emerald x17 / gold x23 while waiting behind an announced Master save. Those exact stacks remained after backend load, graceful backend restart, and graceful Master restart. Result SHA-256 `031CF4C0A0B10EFC22CD12B854773FC82AC2E9B4282ACCB4EDE685593A879B07`; candidate Paperclip SHA-256 `6C65DDC42663B9924566B568153AEA6EF77B63648566850C3170B392AE0CBC4F`; Master SHA-256 `E50B82443F1A1303258B5013DB156F9094CDAA372B043A25A0862462057233DE`. Fixture services shut down gracefully and all six fixture ports were confirmed closed.
- Scope limit: this 273-call run did not change ownership during the burst and did not replay the live workload. Separate old-Master/candidate stale-owner tests already show that an old Master can accept and serve a stale former-owner snapshot, while the tested candidate fence rejects it and preserves the newer chest contents. The original live chest reports remain un-attributed; no live data was opened, changed, or restored.
- **RC5-01 remains P0 `TESTING`, not VERIFIED.** The exact no-holder path and warning-scale read burst pass isolated conservation checks, but the current clean source/package gate, broader unload/write interleavings, and incident attribution remain open. No source change is justified by the warning alone.

Statuses: `REPORTED`, `REPRODUCED`, `FIX IN PROGRESS`, `TESTING`, `VERIFIED`, `BLOCKED`. `VERIFIED` requires a passing test that covers the reported behavior on the applicable candidate. A log match or a narrow adjacent pass is not a fix verification.

### RC5-02 session-bound Master transaction fence — 2026-10-28

- Master-bound playerdata transaction participants now carry the registered player session UUID. Master rejects unregistered, zero, stale-epoch and non-owner transactions before journal writes. Same-owner current-epoch transactions remain supported; cross-owner after-image transactions remain rejected. The transaction-layout capability marker was bumped to `Integer.MIN_VALUE + 3`, leaving session registration at `+2` and message IDs 44/45 stable; an old `+1` acknowledgement cannot authorize the new payload.
- Java 25 focused tests passed: `MasterIntegrationTest` **40/40** and Master protocol suites **9/9** (0 failures/errors/skips). The reconnect regression registers a new epoch on the same backend, proves an old-epoch transaction is rejected with both durable participant images unchanged, then proves the new epoch can commit. Cross-owner rejection is checked with both owners' current epochs registered. Details and XML hashes: `performance-results/rc5-invsee-master-session-fence-20261028.md`.

### RC5-02 backend session-registration ordering audit — 2026-10-28

- Read-only current-source trace: a real player's first Master ownership grant in `MultiPaper.sendPlayerConnect` triggers `registerPlayerSession` only after a successful reply. On Master reconnect, `MultiPaperConnection` restores each real player's ownership; after the session capability challenge it replays ownership and registers online sessions. `RegisterPlayerSessionHandler` and transaction handling both use `PlayerDataOperationQueue`, so registration and a future transaction for the same player are ordered at Master. Master rejects registration unless that connection currently owns the player and the session is non-zero/currently connected.
- This closes an important Master-side authentication prerequisite but does **not** make the InvSee edit atomic. Registration's backend round-trip and reconnect replay have no Paper/Master runtime regression in the current candidate; the existing session tests exercise Master-side acceptance/rejection, while no click path invokes the transaction endpoint. A click immediately after login would currently remain fail-closed; that is safe behavior, not functional parity.
- **Status:** RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`. Next meaningful verification must cover the exact backend↔Master negotiation, first join and reconnect session registration, then implement owner attestation and editor cursor participation before enabling edits. Do not relax the same-owner transaction check or the remote click gate.
- Attempted the current backend sender compile in a fresh C: copy of the known-good Paperweight fixture. The copy contained Paperweight task-cache Git pointers linked to shared worktree metadata; `applyAllPatches` ended at `filterPaperApiFromPaper` with JGit `RepositoryNotFoundException`, before compilation (**0 tests**). The Gradle wrapper also lacked a Main-Class manifest, so installed Gradle 9.8 was used. Details and SHA-256 input checks: `performance-results/rc5-session-backend-paperweight-attempt-20261028.md`. Treat current backend session registration/reconnect code as **uncompiled/unverified** until a genuinely isolated cache/worktree succeeds.
- Follow-up used the original registered C: fixture and preserved its previous test outputs before overlay. Direct compile stopped earlier at `multipaper-api:compileJava` with 100 missing Bukkit symbols (generated Paper API classpath not prepared); explicit `applyAllPatches` then independently reproduced the missing `filterPaperApiFromPaper` task-cache repository error. In both attempts the backend compiler was not reached and **0 tests ran**. The current source copies and fixture backup are described in the same report. Do not label the session sender verified; resume after the missing Paperweight cache provenance is reconciled.

## RC5-01 — Chest contents disappearing

- **Severity / status:** P0 data integrity / `TESTING` (fix candidate validated only in isolated fixtures; real-player cause unknown).
- **Symptoms:** User reports multiple players' chests became empty. This could be actual persisted item loss or a stale/unloaded backend/client view. Neither supplied real backend log contains chest or container inventory diagnostics.
- **Reproduction:** In a newly generated exact-RC4 two-backend fixture, seed one chest with 32 diamonds and a double chest with 17 emeralds plus 23 gold. After the tested paired chunk unload/restart sequence, both block entity `Items` lists were empty in Master-backed chunk NBT while chest block states remained. Single-backend unload/reload and a separate 41-diamond chest through a B-only restart retained items. A paired old-Master/fenced-Master fixture later combined ownership handoff, null-holder fallback reads, delayed stale snapshot writes and restarts; exact results are in `performance-results/rc5-crafty1-moonrise-fallback-log-analysis-20261028.md`.
- **Evidence:** That report decodes `region/r.31.31.mca`, local chunk index 264, and distinguishes this isolated persisted NBT loss from a stale view. The supplied logs are hashed and scoped in `performance-results/rc5-real-backend-log-analysis-20261027.md`; they neither confirm nor refute live loss.
- **Confirmed isolated root cause:** `NewChunkHolder.unloadStage2` starts and awaits Paper's async chunk write, then unloads the `LevelChunk`; MultiPaper's later unload callback used to serialize that now-unloaded chunk again. The first snapshot contained all 32 seeded diamonds; the second snapshot had `block_entities=[]` and Master accepted it, replacing the good image. This is specifically an empty post-unload resave, not evidence that the real players' reports had this cause.
- **Fix status:** Candidate change passes the completed `chunkWrite` future into MultiPaper's unload callback. Ownership release/unsubscribe now happens on the tick thread only after successful write completion; failure logs and retains the lock. The post-persisted-unload path does not serialize the already-unloaded chunk a second time. Other loaded-chunk unlocks preserve direct persistence. Source changes are in `MultiPaper.java`, `MultiPaperChunkHandler.java`, generated-source patch `0004`, canonical `0016`, and the existing chunk-handler test suite. No live world was accessed or restored.
- **Regression tests:** Isolated Java 25 focused suite passed **4/4**, zero failures/errors/skips, including the existing two tests plus delayed-completion and failed-write cases. ### Crafty1 Moonrise no-holder chunk-read warning sub-investigation — 2026-10-28

- **Evidence:** Read-only `C:\Users\elibo\Downloads\latest.log`, SHA-256 `920B665985CB2066D2199F0A6795C438D268FEADE91E8BE1246EAEA00CCA25D1`, reports 273 warnings from 20:09:52–20:09:53 across `world,region` chunk coordinates x=-29..34, z=1..35. This is the Crafty1 log identifying `26.3-DEV-HEAD@2265a5a` / API `26.3-RC4`. Original log remains unchanged.
- **Source behavior / suspected cause:** `MultiPaper.readRegionFileAsync` invokes `forceReadChunk` when no local chunk holder exists. That sends `ForceReadChunkMessage` to Master; `ForceReadChunkHandler` reads Master region storage through `RegionFileCache` after waiting on the Master chunk lock. It does not read backend-local region data. “Straight from disk” means Master-backed storage in this path. This intentional fallback does not itself prove stale data or corruption; missing/stale prior writes remain a separate possible cause.
- **Severity/status:** P0 potential data-integrity gate under RC5-01 / `TESTING`. The log has no chest-specific diagnostics and does not confirm corruption.

All four returned exact Master NBT for single chest (32 diamonds) and double chest (17 emeralds, 23 gold ingots). Subsequent chunk load and graceful Backend B restart retained contents. Fixture has Dynmap, which suppressed warnings in the first 20:58 run; later runs did emit no-holder warnings. Every probe directly asserted the null-holder condition, so warning count is supplementary evidence. Master exited gracefully; zero test listeners remained.
- **Evidence location/result:** `D:\multipaper-tests\system-temp\rc5-noholder-chest-20261027-203932\fallback-probe-20261027-205758\RESULT.txt`, backend/Master logs, RCON transcript, source plugin, and preserved prior generated probe JAR (SHA-256 `415BE782375BD94E8F93B800D076AEF3F6B97B5F33CE84E37B15B40EDBEC36F7`). ### Delayed old-owner chest snapshot regression — 2026-10-28

- **Reproduction:** Extended `MasterIntegrationTest` with subscribed fake backends A/B. A owns chunk (31,0), B waits for ownership, A announces an asynchronous save, ownership transfers to B, B persists a chest chunk containing 17 diamonds, and then A sends its delayed stale chest snapshot containing 32 diamonds. Before the fix, the Master acknowledged A's late write (`expected false but was true`), confirming that the old snapshot could replace B's newer image. This demonstrates a data-integrity race in Master write acceptance, not proof that the supplied Crafty incident used this precise sequence.
- **Fix candidate:** `ChunkSubscriptionManager.runIfOwnerOrUnowned` executes region-write validation and queues the Master storage write while holding the same per-chunk lock used for ownership changes. Writes to chunks with no current owner remain accepted for bootstrap compatibility. A non-owner write is rejected, its `WillSaveChunkLaterMessage` read barrier is released, and it cannot replace the current owner's stored region snapshot. The regression verifies exact persisted chest NBT and all 17 diamond items after the delayed 32-diamond write is rejected.
- **Verification:** With Temurin Java 25.0.3.9 and Gradle 9.8, full `MasterIntegrationTest` passed **31/31**, 0 failures/errors/skips; the complete Master module passed **58/58 across 8 suites**, 0 failures/errors/skips. Two sequential clean Master-module passes rebuilt the shaded JAR byte-identically: SHA-256 `6C7A05021D9C55E9891ABCC8F2FBF2124C984F727DAADFE9A6CFDD156CD705F7` (19,181,802 bytes). Existing unowned-chunk write, failed-write preservation, read-barrier, overlap and disconnect-release tests also passed. `git diff --check` passed. An earlier failed suite run placed this new test after the test that intentionally disconnects backend A; ordering was corrected so the regression runs before crash recovery. No live service was contacted.
- **Limitations / status:** RC5-01 remains P0 `TESTING`. Runtime Paper/Master integration now complements the Master regression: against old Master, former owner A's 32-diamond snapshot was accepted after B saved 17; after disposable B crashed, its restart loaded 32 and the stale state survived Master restart. Against fenced Master, the same delayed snapshot was rejected, B read 17 after restart, and the count remained 17 after Master restart. Separate tests cover direct no-holder reads and exact single/double chest contents. Both ownership orders have now been exercised on old-Master and fenced-Master arms, with concurrent no-holder reads and delayed stale writes. Repeated timing schedules remain open; the live incident cause remains unclassified. The 273 real-log warnings still do not establish live loss or corruption; no evidence supports automatic restoration.

### Former-owner write after final unsubscribe — 2026-10-28

- **Reproduction and cause:** Source review found that the ownerless-write tombstone was removed when the final subscriber unsubscribed. A still-connected former owner could then submit a delayed snapshot, and Master would treat the chunk like a never-owned bootstrap write. This is a separate ordering from the prior test where a subscriber remained. The cause is confirmed in the authorization logic; no live incident is attributed to it.
- **Fix candidate:** `ChunkSubscriptionManager` now retains a per-chunk previous-owner tombstone while that connection remains live, rejects unowned writes to previously owned chunks, and retires tombstones attributed to a backend when it disconnects. A later backend can claim the chunk and write normally. First-ever unowned region writes remain allowed.
- **Regression and result:** Java 25 / Gradle 9.8 focused run of `MasterIntegrationTest.delayedFormerOwnerWriteIsRejectedAfterLastSubscriberUnsubscribes` plus `writtenChunkIsReadableAndVisibleAsLoaded`: **2 passed, 0 failed/errors/skipped**. The new test writes 17 diamonds, unlocks and unsubscribes the last backend, verifies a delayed 32-diamond write is rejected and stored NBT remains x17, then verifies a new owner can write x23. The existing test confirms first-write unowned bootstrap remains supported. An initial all-class attempt had 34 passes and one fixture-order failure because earlier tests close shared `serverA`; the regression now uses fresh clients. After final owner-attribution changes, two sequential clean full Master-module runs each passed **70/70**; their clean shaded JARs matched at 19,201,746 bytes, SHA-256 `2BE4B169A570543A5AFBCC7B80767336F27ACE8AFAD74EAF5E855853CD3D35DE`. Details: `performance-results/rc5-ownerless-last-unsubscribe-fence-20261028.md`.
- **Status / limits:** RC5-01 remains P0 `TESTING`. Master-module tests/package reproducibility do not prove Paper/Moonrise unload ordering, repeated restart behavior after this exact final-unsubscribe sequence, or cause/recovery of live chest reports. Full-project clean builds/tests and Paperweight patch application remain outstanding; no RC5 Paperclip/release artifact is verified by this change.

## RC5-02 — InvSee++ duplication

- **Severity / status:** P0 data integrity / `BLOCKED` pending an end-to-end owner-validated transaction and testable persistence policy.
- **Symptoms:** Concurrent cross-backend InvSee++ viewers may duplicate or lose an item. Crafty1 logged three stale replacements for `wuzbeq` at 15:06:03, 15:06:09, and 15:06:17; Crafty2 logged `/inventorysee wuzbeq` at 15:05:57 and 15:06:30. The stale-slot handler said source items were absent and it re-synced authoritative slots. These messages alone do not prove duplication/loss.
- **Reproduction:** Protocol-consistent repeat is documented in `performance-results/rc5-invsee-protocol-valid-concurrency-20261027.md`. Two cross-owner clients viewed target slot 0 with 16 diamonds and sent a left-click pickup with 26.3 hashed-slot prediction `changedSlots=[{location:0,item:null}]` and the 16-diamond cursor stack. After closing both windows and cleanly stopping both backends, Master-backed playerdata held 16 diamonds on each editor and zero on the target (16→32). The earlier report's simultaneous probe had an invalid prediction (`changedSlots: []`, `cursorItem: null`) and is superseded for protocol-validity claims.
- **Adjacent baseline controls:** The exact-RC4 single-editor cross-owner shift-click/stack-merge once conserved 16 diamonds (target 8 + viewer 8 → target 0 + viewer 16). A separate exact-RC4 drag-split once conserved 8 diamonds (target slot 0 stack 8 → target slots 1 and 2 stacks 4 each; both owner and mirror agreed). Both are one-shot synthetic controls with no concurrent editor, retry/replay, reconnect, restart, or candidate fix; they show those individual mechanics can work, not that the reproduced simultaneous-pickup defect is fixed. Full steps: `performance-results/rc5-investigation-triage-20261027.md`.
- **Evidence:** Backend B emitted one stale replacement rejection; exact RC4 Paperclip/Master and official InvSee++ hashes, probe hash, run UUIDs, and server log hashes are recorded in `performance-results/rc5-invsee-protocol-valid-concurrency-20261027.md`. Filtered post-shutdown NBT is in `D:\multipaper-tests\system-temp\rc5-concurrent-invsee-20261027\playerdata-protocol-valid-20261027.txt` (SHA-256 recorded in that report). A same-event-turn control on official stock Paper 26.3 build 227 with the same plugin and protocol-valid probe persisted exactly 16 total diamonds (target 0, viewer A 16, viewer B 0), contrasting with the exact-RC4 two-backend result 16→32. Full hashes and limitations: `performance-results/rc5-invsee-stock-paper-control-20261028.md`. This narrows the defect to the tested MultiPaper cross-backend path; it does not certify all InvSee++ use. The supplied real Crafty logs show stale replacements, not human-player item totals.
- **Suspected root cause:** Source inspection and the valid reproduction point to a missing rollback for the editor action after its stale target-inventory replacement is rejected. `updateInventoryWithoutMarkingDirty` marks only the target slot for authoritative resync and returns; it does not identify/correct the originating editor's carried stack. The resulting behavior is consistent with each viewer later depositing its picked-up stack into its own inventory. A targeted fix must correlate the rejected transaction to its editor without clearing unrelated cursors/items; confirm this with a pre/post-fix regression.
- **Protocol constraints confirmed on 2026-10-28:** Local container clicks are applied by the viewer backend's menu; `handleContainerEvent` only forwards clicks when `player.openContainer` is a separate MultiPaper remote-container session. Normal InvSee++ menu clicks instead update the `ExternalPlayer` mirror and later produce coalesced `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacket` slot snapshots. That packet has player/session/owner identity but no click/action ID, source menu, cursor before/after, or acknowledgement. The existing `PlayerDataTransactionManager` is not called from this path and does not by itself make a live multi-player/cursor transaction. Peer packet IDs are positional in `ExternalServerPacketSerializer`; inserting an unconditional packet can desynchronize RC4 peers. Any replacement protocol must use explicit capability negotiation, preserve mixed-version RC4 behavior, and cover cursor escrow/restart cuts before enabling edits.
- **Fix status:** The current fail-closed hook is in the working tree and canonical patch. Exact InvSee++ 0.31.22 runtime checks on the current clean-built pair passed one remote main-inventory click, one remote Ender Chest click, and one owner/remote competing-click schedule; the remote clicks were denied, menus/cursors resynced, and aggregate items remained exactly 16 in the tested schedule. This verifies containment for those cases only. It disables remote editing and does not restore one-server behavior. The intended click-identified, owner-attested commit/ACK-or-NACK/conditional rollback protocol is not implemented; the original exact-RC4 16→32 duplication remains unfixed. RC5-02 stays P0 `BLOCKED`.
- **Code-level blocker:** `MultiPaperInventoryHandler.markDirty` records only a component in a `LinkedHashSet`; `tick` coalesces dirty slots into `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacket`, losing click/editor provenance. The owner handler applies slots individually and cannot atomically validate all before-images or ACK/NACK one click. The editor cursor/playerdata and remote target playerdata have no demonstrated crash-atomic commit boundary. A packet-only change cannot safely fix this.

### Fresh source-path clarification — 2026-10-28

- `handleContainerEvent` forwards a click only when `player.openContainer != null` (a MultiPaper remote-container session). A locally opened InvSee++ menu follows normal `ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.handleContainerClick` processing, so the originating click is not represented by `PlayerActionPacket`; later slot deltas are coalesced by inventory component and sent separately. The exact RC4 valid-click reproduction matches this attribution gap.
- The temporary 0153 hook is called after `handleContainerEvent` and before vanilla click mutation. Its API-marker detection now covers InvSee++ 0.31.22 main/ender wrappers. Exact-plugin disposable runtime checks later verified denial/resync for main and Ender Chest clicks and exact 16-item aggregate conservation for one owner/remote competing-click schedule; see `performance-results/rc5-current-pair-invsee-containment-20261028.md`. Do not describe it as the duplication fix or functional cross-backend editing.
- No source change or test was made in this read-only trace. RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`: the next implementation must capture actor/session/click before mutation and coordinate owner validation plus durable target/editor cursor/playerdata commit before ACK.
- **Minimal implementation sequence (corrected 2026-10-28):** First establish version safety for the **MasterBound backend↔Master TCP protocol**. Do not use `HelloPacket.supportedCompressionFlags`: that belongs to the separate peer-to-peer `ExternalServerProtocol`. MasterBound starts with `HelloMessage(name, serverUuid)` and has positional IDs; appending optional payload bytes is not a safe extension. A source-level backward-compatible negotiation candidate exists in the common envelope: RC5 Master sends known `SetSecretMessage` with a reserved negative `Message.transactionId`; an RC5 backend recognizes it and replies with the existing `HelloMessage` carrying the same reserved ID. Exact RC4 source shows known-message dispatch proceeds for unmatched callback IDs and callbacks are normally positive, so RC4 peers appear to ignore the marker; an old Master re-handles Hello and sends the ordinary secret. This is **not binary/runtime verified** and must not be enabled until exact RC4 Master/backend interoperability and callback collision tests pass. If it fails, use a separate versioned endpoint or explicit fail-closed matched-version gate. An RC5 backend sending appended transaction ID 44 to an RC4 Master is unsafe without a proven gate. Then, before vanilla mutation, defer remote-player clicks keyed by editor/session, menu ID, target/session/owner and transaction ID. Acquire owner leases/revisions, revalidate and replay exactly once, capture changed target slots and before-images during replay, and require target-owner attestation plus editor-owner participation before commit/ACK. NACK must return authoritative state. Serialize competing edits and only roll back if editor post-images still match. Clear pending state on close/disconnect/session change/timeout. Define/test a journal or save barrier spanning target and editor playerdata before claiming crash durability.
- **Regression tests:** The isolated baseline has one successful shift-click/stack-merge and one successful drag-split, as noted above; neither is a fix regression or repeated randomized run. The RC5 containment suite now covers main/ender and remote/local/unresolved target predicates, with exact-plugin runtime denial/resync checks. Still missing the functional candidate matrix: protocol-valid simultaneous/replayed/reordered clicks, stale before-images, duplicate proposal handling, cursor and carried-stack conservation, left/right click, repeated shift-click and drag, reconnect/ownership change mid-transaction, peer capability gating, timeout/close cleanup, and durable aggregate counts across backend/Master restart.
- **Probe tooling update:** `scripts/Probe-InvSeeCrossBackend.js` now tracks observed window slots/cursor and sends a predicted changed slot plus hashed carried stack for its supported whole-stack pickup/place operations. `node --check` passes. This makes the existing single-editor probe's packets client-consistent; it is not a concurrent regression, was not run against the live service, and does not verify a fix.
- **Verification result:** The protocol-consistent synthetic pickup reproduces persisted duplication on exact RC4; matching single-server stock Paper control preserved 16 items. The temporary remote-click gate and its focused unit test have not been built or run. This is not a human-client test; RC5-02 remains P0 BLOCKED and remote InvSee parity is not fixed. Do not claim item conservation fixed.

### RC5-02 current transaction boundary audit — 2026-10-28

- The current `PlayerDataTransactionManager` and Master endpoint provide durable, idempotent playerdata after-image replacement guarded by saved-file hashes and registered player sessions. They are groundwork only: `CommitPlayerDataTransactionHandler` requires the sender to own every participant, and no InvSee click calls it.
- A direct caller from the editor backend would be unsafe. Ordinary InvSee menus mutate the editor's local menu and `ExternalPlayer` mirror, then coalesce target slot deltas into `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacket`. That message has no click ID, editor before/after image, cursor escrow, or ACK. The remote target owner does not attest its current live state/revision. Committing a full mirrored playerdata snapshot could overwrite the target owner's newer unsaved inventory; removing the Master ownership restriction would not fix that race.
- Safe next implementation boundary: negotiate protocol capability, identify a single click against both player session epochs, reserve/validate at each live owner on its tick thread, include editor cursor/inventory and target inventory revisions, journal an idempotent commit, apply it on both owners, and ACK only after both live states reconcile. Rejection/timeout/disconnect/restart must leave both participants at a defined before or after state. Keep the click gate closed until competing/replayed/reordered click and crash-cut tests cover cursor and target totals after backend and Master restarts.
- **Current verification:** The exact-plugin fail-closed main/ender and one simultaneous owner/remote runtime schedule passed on the current clean-built pair. This validates only that containment schedule, not functional parity or remote transaction safety. RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`; see `performance-results/rc5-invsee-live-owner-attestation-gap-20261028.md`, `performance-results/rc5-invsee-master-endpoint-trust-boundary-audit-20261028.md`, and `performance-results/rc5-current-pair-invsee-containment-20261028.md`.

This is a source/API compile defect in the unverified containment gate, not a failed conservation assertion.

## RC5-03 — Broken boats

- **Severity / status:** P1 gameplay/crash risk / `TESTING`.
- **Symptoms:** Cross-backend dependency updates throw `ClassCastException` when a boat is cast to `Mob` in `MultiPaperEntitiesHandler.handleEntityWithDependenicesUpdate`.
- **Reproduction:** Direct real-log reproduction: Crafty2 `1latest.log` records 18 Boat→Mob exceptions from 14:59:38 through 15:20:29 at source lines 349/351, with candidate artifact `26.3-DEV-2265a5a`. Fresh disposable runtime test also spawned a boat and pig, mounted the pig, moved the boat, and asserted both backends retained one boat and pig with matching UUIDs and passenger relation.
- **Evidence:** `performance-results/rc5-real-backend-log-analysis-20261027.md`; log hash table therein.
- **Suspected root cause:** `ClientboundSetEntityLinkPacket` is handled as a leash update and unconditionally casts its entity to `Mob`; boats are not mobs. Passenger dependencies use a separate branch.
- **Fix status:** The logged root defect is fixed in source: the entity-link handler now ignores non-`Mob` entities safely, while preserving passenger handling for boats and leash handling for mobs. Treat this exact Boat→Mob cast defect as a verified sub-fix; the broader boat behavior category remains `TESTING` for the lifecycle and client-visible cases below.
- **Regression tests:** `MultiPaperEntitiesHandlerDependencyTest` covers non-Mob/boat entity-link safety, boat passenger dependency, and Mob leash set/clear. The current-candidate isolated Java 25 suite passed 3/3 (0 skipped/failures/errors); XML SHA-256 `51118C65B4C3E57FC83304996C50082A1BD59C8E39D28008B4531901301918B6`.
- **Verification result:** `applyAllPatches` and two sequential clean full builds passed in the isolated Java 25 worktree (each 10,749 tests, 0 failures/errors, 88 skipped; matching output JAR hashes recorded in `performance-results/rc5-boat-fix-reproducible-builds-20261027.md`). In addition, the exact current-candidate Paperclip/Master pair passed a disposable two-backend mount, dismount, remount, A→B ownership transfer, new-owner movement, and former-owner observer check with matching entity UUIDs and no `ClassCastException` or dependency warning. Exact artifact and fixture evidence: `performance-results/rc5-boat-passenger-runtime-20261027.md`. **The original cast failure is verified fixed by its direct unit regression; the broader RC5-03 category stays `TESTING`** because real-player riding/rendering, restart/dimension travel, and deliberately generating the original leash-link packet remain untested.

- **Severity / status:** P1 global state consistency / `TESTING`.

Required next: set/query gamerules on both backends after a rejected/throwing remote update and backend restart.
- **Verification result:** Narrow exception-safety behavior passes tests. The reported cross-backend command/gamerule mismatch remains unverified; do not mark fixed until paired runtime assertions pass.
- **Latest runtime follow-up:** The exact clean-pair gamerule runner also set `players_sleeping_percentage=50` on B, read it on A, and rechecked it on both after backend and Master restarts. The existing `advance_time`, `random_tick_speed`, and `spawn_mobs` assertions passed in the same run. Result/transcript hashes and limits: `performance-results/rc5-sleep-threshold-cleanpair-runtime-20261028.md`. This narrows the global gamerule coverage but does not verify all commands/settings, locator bar, sleep quorum, or night skip; RC5-10 stays P1 `TESTING`.

## RC5-13 — Entity UUID/NBT synchronization errors

- **Severity / status:** P1 entity integrity/visibility / `REPRODUCED` from real diagnostics; multiple cases remain undiagnosed.
- **Symptoms:** Entity NBT updates arrive while entity chunks are unavailable; duplicate UUID insertions, removal-routing fallbacks, invalid entity records and mismatched block-entity state appear in real logs.
- **Reproduction:** Prior Crafty logs contain 78 unloaded-entity-chunk NBT warnings total (65 Crafty2, 13 Crafty1), 9 Crafty2 UUID collision warnings, 26 Crafty2 non-owner removal-route failures with broadcast fallback, 494 invalid-entity skips total (489 Crafty2, 5 Crafty1), and two Crafty2 brushable-block NBT/state mismatches at 15:21:55. New Crafty1 `latest.log` adds 8 UUID collisions, 13 unloaded-entity-chunk NBT warnings and 10 non-owner removal-route failures with broadcast fallback. These are diagnostic reports, not proof of entity loss; details for the new log are in `performance-results/rc5-crafty1-moonrise-fallback-log-analysis-20261028.md`.
- **Evidence:** Logs are only diagnostic evidence; each class needs source-path correlation and a deterministic reproducer. The exact current code drops an incoming entity update when the destination entity chunk is unavailable and sets a short-lived removal marker; logs do not establish whether that path explains each record. Earlier UUID canonicalization and entity removal routing suites passed on an older current-tip source revision, but the new live logs are a regression signal and require a changed-scenario repro.
- **Suspected root cause:** Multiple possible races: entity update vs chunk readiness, stale ownership/chunk subscriptions, UUID registration across adjacent chunks, removal routing when the owner disconnects, or malformed entity chunk NBT. No single root cause is established.
- **Fix status:** No new fix for these log events. Existing entity sync fixes remain in history; do not repeat old tests unless one is specific to a newly evidenced path.
- **Regression tests:** Existing `EntityUpdateNBTPacket`, request entity and removal routing tests are documented in prior reports. Missing are tests reproducing real log patterns: update before destination chunk load, entity crossing adjacent chunks with duplicate UUID arrival, non-owner remove with unavailable owner route, and invalid NBT/block entity mismatch classification.
- **Verification result:** Current supplied candidate emits the recorded warnings; entity UUID/NBT integrity remains an RC5 blocker pending scoped regressions and results.

## Update — 2026-10-27 — InvSee rejection path trace

- Exact-RC4 source review confirms `MultiPaperInventoryHandler.updateInventoryWithoutMarkingDirty` rejects a stale replacement when the replaced stack is no longer in the authoritative player's inventory/cursor, then marks only the authoritative target slot for resync. The external inventory packet carries the target player UUID/session/owner and slot snapshots, but no originating editor/action identity. `handleContainerEvent` forwards the editor's click packet to its external owner and returns; there is no transaction result routed back to that editor.
- This matches the observed failure shape: the owner can reject a target-slot mutation after the remote `/invsee` menu has already predicted a cursor stack, while the editor replica is not identified for compensation. It is a source-correlated root-cause lead, not yet a fully isolated proof that no other path contributes.
- No code change made. A safe fix needs transaction correlation and a conditional correction on the originating editor replica; blindly clearing cursors or subtracting inventory items could destroy legitimate items. Regression must assert exact aggregate counts over repeated mixed clicks, two simultaneous editors, close/reconnect and graceful persistence before `VERIFIED`.
- Report runner subsection corrected to record the successful exact-RC4 integration rerun and supersede its earlier non-concurrent attempt. RC5-02 remains `REPRODUCED`/P0.

## Update — 2026-10-27 — duplicate leave-message source trace

- Read current PlayerList and disconnect synchronization patches plus `PlayerRemovePacket`. PlayerList fires `PlayerQuitEvent` only for real players; external replicas do not fire the normal quit event. External replica removal also checks that the sending connection is still that replica's current owner before disconnecting it. Thus a routine mirrored-player removal should not independently emit a second vanilla quit announcement.
- This does not identify the user's duplicate message source. The Crafty logs only record server-side join/quit/disconnect events and do not capture what a client received. No controlled real-client disconnect, owner transfer, proxy reconnect, or duplicate-broadcast reproduction was performed, and no fix was made.
- RC5-12 stays `REPORTED`; required next evidence is one controlled client-visible disconnect with timestamped chat packet capture and correlated backend/proxy logs, separating backend transition announcements from a true duplicate quit broadcast.

## Update — 2026-10-27 — InvSee transaction identity boundary

- Added `performance-results/rc5-invsee-transaction-identity-source-trace-20261027.md` after a read-only inspection of the patch click hook, inventory dirty-slot coalescing and `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacket`. Updates identify only the edited target session/component/slot; the tick flush loses editor/action attribution. The stale-owner rejection can resync the target but cannot safely undo a specific editor's predicted cursor state.
- No product change or test was made. A speculative cursor clear or full inventory restore would risk deleting valid later items. RC5-02 remains `REPRODUCED`/P0. Gate the fix on correlated, acknowledged transaction semantics plus conservation assertions over concurrent clicks, close, ownership transition and restart. Wire-format changes also need explicit mixed-version behavior because packet IDs are positional.

- Additional wire review: `ExternalServerPacketDecoder` uses a positional packet ID and invokes the decoder on the rest of the frame without a trailing-byte/extension boundary. Therefore neither appending fields to the current inventory packet nor sending a newly appended packet ID to an old peer is safe without explicit protocol capability negotiation/fail-closed handling. No packet changes were made; mixed-version compatibility is now an explicit design gate.

- **Correction to earlier compatibility design lead:** `HelloPacket.supportedCompressionFlags` is in `ExternalServerProtocol`, not `MasterBoundProtocol`; it cannot negotiate support for the MasterBound `CommitPlayerDataTransactionMessage`. MasterBound `HelloMessage` carries only backend name and server UUID. The transaction message is appended at positional ID 44, preserving older IDs but not making ID 44 safe to send to an RC4 Master. A separate versioned endpoint or hard matched-version gate remains a fallback. A different candidate using the common `Message.transactionId` field on existing known messages may allow backward-compatible negotiation, but exact RC4 binary behavior and callback-ID collision safety must be tested before use. The earlier reserved-bit idea remains only a peer-to-peer protocol observation.


## Update — 2026-10-27 — gamerule suppression failure hardening

- Source inspection found `GameRuleUpdatePacket.handle` set a static suppression flag before calling Bukkit and reset it only on normal return. A thrown exception could leave later local gamerule changes permanently unbroadcast on that process. Changed the helper to restore its prior value in `finally`, preserving nested scope state. Canonical patch `patches/server/0040-Sync-gamerules.patch` and generated source were updated together.

## Update — 2026-10-27 — InvSee++ RC4 wire compatibility source audit

- Compared the exact Crafty-log RC4 source commit `2265a5a3bf54d1a482c5ac21bd856a9a2674ee32`: its peer-to-peer Hello frame already carries `supportedCompressionFlags` in a VarInt and compression negotiation masks only ZLIB/ZSTD bits. This supports an unknown capability bit only for that peer-to-peer protocol; it does not negotiate MasterBound transaction support and is not a runtime compatibility result.
- RC4's external packet decoder uses positional IDs without skipping unconsumed fields. No RC5-only packet or extension can be sent to RC4 safely unless the new capability is verified on that exact peer connection and send paths fail closed.
- No source/wire change was made. Required next: capability unit tests plus disposable mixed exact-RC4/RC5 peer handshake and reconnect tests before implementing/sending correlated inventory transactions. RC5-02 remains P0 `REPRODUCED`. Detail: `performance-results/rc5-invsee-transaction-identity-source-trace-20261027.md`.

## Update — 2026-10-28 — RC5 Moonrise fallback and related Crafty1 warnings

- **Read-only evidence:** Crafty1 log SHA-256 `920B665985CB2066D2199F0A6795C438D268FEADE91E8BE1246EAEA00CCA25D1` (110,461 bytes), identified as `26.3-DEV-HEAD@2265a5a` / API `26.3-RC4`. The recorded 273 `has no chunk holder for reading chunk` warnings all occur on Paper I/O Worker #0 during 20:09:52–20:09:53, for `world,region` chunks x=-29..34 and z=1..35. No chest/block-entity context is attached to those warnings; the original input was not edited.
- **Moonrise/source conclusion:** On the exact RC4 source path, `MultiPaper.readRegionFileAsync` takes the no-holder branch and calls `forceReadChunk`, which sends `ForceReadChunkMessage`. Master `ForceReadChunkHandler` reads its own world `region` through `RegionFileCache`, after waiting on the Master chunk lock. Thus this branch is a deliberate Master-backed fallback, not a backend-local disk bypass. The warning text is imprecise. A fallback can still return stale data if Master already holds a stale snapshot; the warning cannot establish freshness or corruption.
- **Chest regression evidence:** Disposable Java 25 Paper/Master tests explicitly forced a null local holder with populated single and double chests, ran four concurrent fallback reads under a pending-save barrier, and checked exact stacks after chunk load plus backend and Master restarts. A second runtime race combined four no-holder reads with a delayed former-owner stale write in both ownership directions. Old-Master controls accepted/persisted the stale 32-diamond image after the new owner saved 17; fenced-Master candidate rejected it and retained 17. Double-chest emerald x17 / gold x23 persisted. Result hashes and fixture/log paths are in `performance-results/rc5-crafty1-moonrise-fallback-log-analysis-20261028.md`. These tests isolate a stale-write race in the old Master path and validate its candidate fence; they do not attribute the players' reports to that race or recover live items. RC5-01 remains P0 `TESTING`.
- **Other log clusters/source interpretation:** 119 `moved too quickly` warnings from 16:27:01–16:27:07 concern one session and are Paper movement-validation messages; the excerpt alone cannot distinguish server corrections/latency from invalid movement and does not establish data loss. Eight duplicate-UUID attempts are entity registration collisions; current `EntityUpdateNBTPacket` logic checks for an existing canonical UUID and updates it, while an unloaded entity chunk causes the candidate to be dropped with a temporary removal marker. Ten removal-routing warnings indicate the receiving backend could not route a request to a connected current owner and used the logged fallback; they are not themselves proof an entity was deleted. Thirteen early entity-NBT updates are consistent with an entity chunk not being ready; current code drops that candidate on the unavailable-chunk branch. All three classes still need focused lifecycle regressions and are tracked under RC5-13; these source paths do not prove the live cases are harmless.
- **InvSee linkage:** Three stale inventory replacement rejections at 15:06:03, :09 and :17 are warning/guard events, not item totals. The independent protocol-valid disposable reproduction persists a 16-to-32 diamond duplication against exact RC4 and preserves 16 in stock Paper control. RC5-02 remains P0 `REPRODUCED` with no production fix; see its tracker entry and `performance-results/rc5-invsee-protocol-valid-concurrency-20261027.md`.
- **Open gates:** The two ownership directions and stale-write/no-holder interleaving have been exercised, but varied timing schedules and broader unload/save sequences remain. No live SMP files, processes, or worlds were accessed. No recovery is claimed.

### RC5-02 cursor crash-safety requirement — 2026-10-28

- **Source finding:** The click path consults `player.containerMenu.getCarried()` in `MultiPaperInventoryHandler`, while the player-file synchronization path persists `player.saveWithoutId(...)` (`patches/server/0020-Sync-player-files.patch`). The carried menu cursor is not represented by the normal synchronized player-inventory slot updates. Therefore an ACK for a pickup that leaves items on the cursor cannot by itself guarantee crash-safe conservation: a process failure before the cursor is placed or settled can lose the only runtime copy.
- **Required transaction behavior:** Treat cursor contents as transaction escrow. Do not acknowledge a pickup until its cursor value is durable under an idempotent transaction ID and recoverable exactly once after backend or Master restart. On reconnect, settle escrow into the player's inventory or a safe recovery destination without duplicating a later cursor/slot update. The journal must cover this alongside target and editor playerdata before/after states.
- **Evidence/status:** This is source-path analysis extending the existing independently persisted playerdata boundary; no new runtime crash-cut test or code fix was made. Add forced-stop cuts after pickup, commit, each playerdata write and before ACK, then assert aggregate item totals including cursor/escrow after recovery. RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`.

It can only return stale contents if the authoritative Master snapshot is stale or a separate save/order defect has supplied stale data. The warning itself is not proof of stale data or chest loss.
- **Chest data-integrity regression status:** Existing isolated Java 25 fixtures populated single and double chests, forced `holder=null`, ran four concurrent Master reads, overlapped pending/delayed writes, changed ownership in both directions, and checked exact item lists across backend and Master restarts. Old-Master controls accepted and later returned the former owner's x32 snapshot after the new owner stored x17; the fenced candidate rejected that stale write and retained x17, with the double chest unchanged. These bounded tests passed; they establish a reproducible stale-write hazard in the old Master path and candidate protection for those schedules, not causation of any live report. Full unload/reload lifecycle coverage and production incident attribution remain open.
- **Other warnings / status:** Retained analysis finds no temporal or coordinate evidence tying the movement messages to chests. The 119 movement warnings are movement-validation diagnostics. Duplicate UUID, unloaded-chunk NBT, and removal-routing warnings are separate entity lifecycle/routing symptoms; source branches and count evidence are documented under RC5-13, but their requested focused regressions remain open. The three stale inventory rejections indicate the handler refused stale replacements and requested authoritative resync; they do not establish item conservation, and the independent InvSee++ 16→32 reproduction remains RC5-02 P0 `BLOCKED`.
- **Tracker statuses:** RC5-01 remains P0 `TESTING` (potential integrity issue, not confirmed corruption); RC5-13 remains P1 `REPRODUCED` (log symptoms reproduced from preserved evidence, fixes/regressions unverified); RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`. ### RC5-02 playerdata durability boundary audit — 2026-10-28

- **Source evidence:** Master `ReadPlayerHandler` reads `world/playerdata/<uuid>.dat` through `FileLocker`; `WritePlayerHandler` replaces only that UUID's file and acknowledges independently. Existing `ContainerTransactionManager` WAL is connected to chunk/entity region reads/writes and identifies block/entity inventory snapshots; it does not cover playerdata files or multi-player commits.
- **Implication for root fix:** Peer-side click correlation and ACK/NACK are necessary to reconcile a rejected editor prediction, but insufficient for durable conservation. If the target owner saves the target change before the editor's corresponding inventory/cursor change (or vice versa) and a process crashes, vanilla-like transfer semantics can still lose or duplicate value. A complete implementation needs a Master-persisted transaction record spanning both UUID playerdata states (or an equivalent commit/recovery protocol), idempotent transaction IDs, owner/session validation, and explicit rules for save/ack ordering. Crash tests must stop after each journal/commit step and recover both files to a consistent before or after state.
- **Status:** RC5-02 stays P0 `BLOCKED`: exact-RC4 16→32 reproduction and stock Paper 16-item control remain; the temporary remote-click gate is not final behavior. No implementation or test was added from this source audit. Detailed traces: `performance-results/rc5-invsee-transaction-identity-source-trace-20261027.md`.

### RC5-02 ownership authority detail — 2026-10-28

Master `PlayerConnectHandler` records each player UUID on one `ServerConnection`, rejects another simultaneous owner, and `PlayerDisconnectHandler` removes it. `ServerConnection.hasPlayer(UUID)` provides the Master-side owner check; existing `MasterIntegrationTest.playerCanOnlyConnectToOneServerAtATime` covers that invariant. Both participating backends would need to validate their current session and reject reconnect/ownership changes between reservation and commit. ### Follow-up — 2026-10-28 — accepted player save during abrupt backend close

Source review found a loss window in the preceding ownership fence: `WritePlayerHandler` checked `connections.contains(connection)` only when its asynchronous per-player operation finally ran. If the ordered write message had already reached Master but its operation was queued, `channelInactive` could remove the backend first and cause Master to reject that valid final save. The handler now captures connection/UUID ownership synchronously when handling the ordered message; the per-UUID queue then places that accepted write before later disconnect/reconnect work. Explicit disconnect marks the UUID as disconnecting immediately, and socket close marks each owned UUID before removing the connection, so later stale writes do not inherit the accepted status.

Added a deterministic integration sequence to `playerCanOnlyConnectToOneServerAtATime`: it seeds playerdata, blocks that UUID's operation queue, sends a replacement save and ordered ping, waits until Master has processed backend `channelInactive`, then releases the queue and reads exact persisted bytes from the peer. This is a focused Master protocol regression; it does not exercise Paper's generated PlayerList final-save hook or a live RC4 logout.

Verification on Temurin Java 25.0.3.9 / Gradle 9.8: the focused player ownership/save test passed **1/1**, the shared-data test that transiently timed out in one preceding full run passed **1/1** standalone, and the subsequent full `MasterIntegrationTest` passed **32/32** (0 failures/errors/skips). One intermediate integration run had 1 timeout in the unrelated shared-data test; it reproduced the known transient path, passed standalone, and the next full integration class passed. RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`: this does not add InvSee editor/action identity, a target/editor atomic transaction, cursor reconciliation, mixed-version capability negotiation, or crash-cut conservation.

### RC5-02 editor-provenance source trace — 2026-10-28

- Current source confirms the protocol gap implicated by the protocol-valid 16→32 reproduction. Local InvSee menu clicks run through vanilla click handling. A mutation to an `ExternalPlayer` target mirror is sent at tick end as a target `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacket` carrying target UUID/session/owner plus slot before/after image; it does not carry the editor UUID/session, click/window/state ID, editor cursor, or transaction ID.
- Target owner checks target session then applies the replacement against its current slot. On stale mismatch it can reject and resync that target slot, but it cannot identify or roll back the clicker's cursor/inventory already mutated on the other backend. This concretely explains why stale-target rejection cannot guarantee total conservation; see `performance-results/rc5-invsee-editor-provenance-source-trace-20261028.md`.
- Required fix boundary: idempotent two-player operation with both owner/session identities, click identity, target before-image, editor cursor/player inventory, serialized ownership validation, journaled after-images and ACK/recovery semantics. Must test simultaneous and replayed edits, reconnect/transfer, both owners, cursor, shift/drag and crash cuts with exact aggregate item counts and stock Paper control.
- No production change in this trace. Patch `0153` remains temporary fail-closed containment, and the Master journal remains unconnected. Exact-RC4 duplication persists; RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`.

### RC5-02 Master transaction endpoint trust-boundary audit — 2026-10-28

- Current `CommitPlayerDataTransactionHandler` source validates that the sender remains connected, both participant UUIDs have active owners matching the supplied backend names, neither is disconnecting, and the sender owns at least one participant. It then journals both supplied playerdata after-images under `PlayerDataOperationQueue.submitAll`.
- `MasterIntegrationTest.durablePlayerTransactionValidatesBothOwnersAndIsIdempotent` checks both-owner name matching, rejection for wrong owner/unowned sender, durable readback, and duplicate transaction ID behavior. This covers Master persistence and admission, not a player inventory click.
- The request still lets one participant's owner supply the other participant's after-image. The other owner does not attest to a current sync-session token, target before-image, or proposed after-image. Since InvSee++ mutates the remote `ExternalPlayer` mirror before its update is sent, this endpoint must not be connected directly to that update path: a durable commit alone could persist a stale mirrored target image. It also has no editor/click identity or cursor escrow.
- **Required integration gate:** both live owners must validate and prepare the same operation against their current sessions/before-images; the operation must serialize competing changes, include cursor and affected editor slots, commit idempotently, then reconcile both live players before client acknowledgement. Add stale-owner, stale-before-image, replay, simultaneous-editor, and crash-cut client-valid tests before allowing remote edits.
- No production caller was added. The unverified 0153 click gate remains a compatibility regression rather than a functional fix. Exact-RC4 protocol-valid 16→32 item duplication remains P0; RC5-02 remains `BLOCKED`.

### RC5-10 gamerule replication failure — 2026-10-28

- **Reproduction:** Exact current persistent-input candidate Paperclip `391D91696B8C6DBBFD1A952FABF15A5F31AA2DB5D129BC742CCC279905438349` and Master `BB20D1935AC6A12A4B2BA0907097779BD268D4330FA8460C945354711704200C` ran in a marked disposable two-backend fixture. Both backends reached ready. A set valid 26.3 rule `advance_time=false`; A immediately queried `false`, while already-running B still queried `true`. Runner/result: `scripts/Test-RC5GlobalGameRules.ps1`, `D:\multipaper-tests\system-temp\rc5-gamerules-current-20261028-livepeer\RESULT.txt`, SHA-256 `ED242353EE1C08B301B927D21F04EF4166D9333F1F56AA31153E2AA936C611A2`; RCON transcript SHA-256 `4BF54DC5D87EC651640CB09837FB90C6C3342F1C4427AF078539A08628B7010A`. - **Root cause:** 26.3's `GameRuleCommand.setRule` routes commands through `CraftEventFactory.handleGameRuleSet`, which applies accepted changes via `level.getGameRules().set`. The built `CraftEventFactory` contains no MultiPaper broadcast call, and the active `multipaper-server/paper-patches` input has no gamerule patch. The old root `patches/server/0040-Sync-gamerules.patch` targets the pre-26.3 `GameRules` / `CraftWorld` paths and is not in the active Paper patch input directory. Therefore command changes do not reach peers. The incoming `GameRuleUpdatePacket` suppression helper alone does not broadcast anything.
- **Fix in progress:** Added the first persistent change to the active `multipaper-server/paper-patches/files/.../CraftEventFactory.java.patch` input. It broadcasts the accepted, post-event value from the shared 26.3 `handleGameRuleSet` path, so gamerule commands and Bukkit setters both use the same hook; inbound peer updates already suppress rebroadcast. Static `git apply --check` against the current generated Paper source passes. Compilation, duplicate-broadcast review, current-candidate two-backend replication and save/restart persistence are still pending.

### 2026-10-28 InvSee transaction protocol wire-ID compatibility correction

- **Finding:** `Protocol` uses message-list position as the wire ID. The in-progress `CommitPlayerDataTransactionMessage` had been inserted in the middle of `MasterBoundProtocol`, which shifted existing IDs beginning at `DownloadFileMessage` and would make existing peers decode later frames incorrectly.
- **Change:** Moved the new message to the end of the registered list and added pinned compatibility assertions for existing IDs 3, 5 and 43 plus new ID 44.
- **Verification:** Isolated Temurin 25.0.3.9 / Gradle 9.8 `:multipaper-mastermessagingprotocol:test --tests puregero.multipaper.mastermessagingprotocol.messages.ProtocolRoundtripTest --offline --no-build-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=2 --console=plain`: **4/4 passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips. XML SHA-256 `7A66382C2E2AB7B045574052D6242E94ACD09DB2DDB1530B7157311C6D369A73`; full evidence: `performance-results/rc5-protocol-wire-id-compatibility-20261028.md`.
- **Build verification:** Two sequential authoritative clean Java 25 full builds passed 10,771 tests each (0 failures/errors, 88 skipped, 192 XML reports); all nine direct module output JARs match. Packaged Master bytecode confirms legacy IDs 3/43 and appended ID 44. Hashes/manifests: `performance-results/rc5-protocol-wire-id-compatibility-20261028.md`.
- **Status/limits:** This is protocol safety groundwork only. No caller connects the Master transaction endpoint to InvSee clicks, and cross-owner commits remain rejected. RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`; no conservation/functional fix is claimed. The current build outputs match source, but the release-stage ZIP still needs regeneration and review after the transaction work is resolved.

### RC5-02 MasterBound capability scope correction — 2026-10-28

The earlier spare `HelloPacket.supportedCompressionFlags` bit proposal was on the wrong wire. That Hello is backend peer-to-peer; the durable transaction endpoint is MasterBound backend-to-Master. MasterBound `HelloMessage` has only name + UUID; `CommitPlayerDataTransactionMessage` is positional ID 44. Appending keeps older IDs stable but an RC4 Master has no decoder for ID 44. No MasterBound capability field/negotiation or matched-version sender gate currently exists. Do not implement or report the peer Hello bit as a transaction capability. Source audit, exact paths and required compatibility gates: `performance-results/rc5-invsee-masterbound-capability-boundary-20261028.md`. No product source/test change was made. RC5-02 remains P0 BLOCKED.

### RC5-02 capability gate implementation and focused regression — 2026-10-28

- **Current source clarification:** The reserved envelope-marker handshake is now implemented in the current worktree (Master challenge via existing `SetSecretMessage`; backend ACK via existing `HelloMessage`). `MultiPaperConnection` blocks transaction sends until the ACK; Master rejects transactions from unnegotiated connections. These changes supersede older tracker notes saying the source implementation itself was absent, but do not supersede their exact-RC4 runtime-probe limitations.
- **Regression results:** Java 25 / Gradle 9.8 protocol tests passed 8/8 (0 failures/errors/skips), including marker round trips in both wire directions and stable IDs. Master `MasterIntegrationTest` passed 37/37 (0 failures/errors/skips), including an unacknowledged legacy-style peer whose two-participant transaction was rejected and neither player image was persisted, plus repeated-Hello renegotiation before re-ACK with the same no-persistence assertion. Existing same-owner durability and cross-owner rejection tests passed too. XML hashes, exact command, and fixture cleanup: `performance-results/rc5-masterbound-capability-handshake-tests-20261028.md`.
- **Attempt history:** An uncached run stalled in `shadowJar` archive traversal in Windows `File.exists` calls before tests; only that Gradle task was stopped. The first focused test attempt failed because its fixture created one participant although the protocol requires exactly two; the test was corrected and the passing result is separate. The successful focused run excluded only `:MultiPaper-Master:shadowJar`.
- **Follow-up hardening:** Test review found a repeated ordinary Hello could retain a previous capability grant. Master now clears the grant on every new Hello; the 37/37 integration result above covers refusal until a fresh ACK.
- **Limits/status:** This is only the MasterBound safety handshake and durable Master endpoint groundwork. No Paper/InvSee++ click caller, remote owner prepare/attest, cursor/inventory reconciliation, mixed RC4/RC5 runtime negotiation, reconnect regression, or item conservation implementation exists. Remote InvSee remains denied by containment; the exact-RC4 16→32 duplicate is not fixed. RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`; do not mark verified or release.

### RC5-02 Ender Chest packet ordering audit — 2026-10-28

- **Symptoms/evidence:** The retained Crafty1 log has three stale main-inventory replacement rejections during the recorded InvSee++ activity (15:06:03, 15:06:09, 15:06:17). Those warnings show stale updates were rejected/resynced; they do not report aggregate item totals or prove those particular stacks were duplicated/lost. The separate exact-RC4 protocol-valid two-editor reproduction still demonstrates 16 diamonds becoming 32, and stock Paper control retained 16.
- **Source/root-cause lead:** Main inventory packets carry a `Replacing` before-image and the target owner compares it against the current slot. Ender Chest updates from `MultiPaperEnderChestHandler` do not include that before-image; their receiver directly sets the Ender Chest slot and refreshes its cache. Delayed/out-of-order Ender Chest updates therefore lack the same stale-slot fence. This is a potential consistency weakness, not evidence of an observed Ender Chest loss.
- **Reproduction/regression status:** No deterministic stale Ender Chest reorder reproduction or stale-write rejection test exists. `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacketTest` covers packet round-trip only. The exact InvSee++ 0.31.22 `/endersee` isolated runtime test passed the fail-closed gate case (target EnderItems x16 remained x16; client cursor was empty after denial/resync); it does not test accepted edits or reordering. Source trace and limitations: `performance-results/rc5-invsee-editor-provenance-source-trace-20261028.md` and `performance-results/rc5-invsee-ender-click-runtime-20261028.md`.
- **Status:** RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`. Main-inventory stale rejection and EnderSee click denial are containment only; cross-owner transaction identity, owner attestation, cursor reconciliation, Ender Chest ordering, and repeated/concurrent/restart item conservation remain open. No source fix was made in this audit.

### RC5-02 playerdata read ordering regression — 2026-10-28

- **Reproduced source defect:** `ReadPlayerHandler` performed independent asynchronous playerdata reads while player writes, ownership changes, and durable transactions are serialized by `PlayerDataOperationQueue`. A read issued after an earlier queued save could overtake it and expose the old image.
- **Regression:** `MasterIntegrationTest.playerReadWaitsForEarlierQueuedSave` deliberately blocks the UUID queue, sends a save and then a read in channel order, and asserts the read waits and returns the saved bytes. With the old handler, the test failed: expected first byte `7`, observed stale byte `1`. After the fix, it passed.
- **Fix:** Player reads now enter the same per-player queue using the parsed UUID. This orders reads with writes/transactions and ownership transitions for the tested UUID.
- **Verification:** Disposable Java 25.0.3.9 worktree, two sequential clean Master module builds/tests (`clean test shadowJar`), each **73/73 passed across 10 suites**, zero failures/errors/skips. Shaded Master JARs matched: 19,202,357 bytes, SHA-256 `29140A8B49ADBDE1277A9E8BB5C01CC6834C0471664093EFC81402E8746F9766`. Integration XML SHA-256 `ACB2A4C4DA2570632827293ABEF6093B3EC9F7B39601EC336D744F7304CF742C`; full report `performance-results/rc5-player-read-after-save-ordering-20261028.md`.
- **Limits/status:** This is a verified Master playerdata ordering fix for the tested queued-save/read schedule. It is only supporting inventory consistency work; no remote owner attestation, InvSee click transaction, cursor escrow, or cross-backend conservation regression is integrated. RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`; no InvSee fix is claimed. No live system accessed.

### RC5-02 Master transaction before-image fence — 2026-10-28

- **Change/root cause addressed:** The Master transaction journal accepted full participant after-images without proving they were based on the current durable playerdata images. The new capability-gated transaction participant requires a 32-byte SHA-256 before-image, and the capability marker has been bumped to a distinct version-2 value so peers that negotiated the earlier after-image-only format are not considered compatible. Master checks all before-images under the world transaction lock, after UUID serialization and before writing the journal; one stale participant rejects the whole batch. Exact duplicate committed IDs remain idempotent. Same-backend ownership validation remains; cross-backend requests are still fail-closed.
- **Regression results:** Explicit Java 25 / Gradle 9.8 fresh focused invocation passed **46/46**: protocol tests 8/8 and Master integration 38/38. The integration test verifies a stale second participant rejects the batch without changing either player file, fresh hashes commit both, old capability acknowledgements do not unlock the changed layout, and replay after later save does not restore old images. XML hashes and source hashes: `performance-results/rc5-invsee-beforeimage-master-fence-20261028.md`.
- **Attempt history/limits:** A wider module run completed 73 tests but 5 child-process restart tests failed because the preceding `shadowJar` traversal stalled and was stopped before producing a valid child Master artifact. The captured thread dump is `performance-results/rc5-master-beforeimage-thread-dump-20261028.txt`; those failures are retained and not called passing. No packaged JAR or two-clean-build result is claimed. The Paper/InvSee click path still has no transaction caller, cursor/session reconciliation, target-owner prepare/attestation, or accepted cross-owner conservation test. Exact-RC4 duplication is not fixed.
- **Status:** RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`; this is durable endpoint safety groundwork only. Remote InvSee edits remain disabled. No live server/world was accessed.


## RC5-01 ownership handoff waits for pending chunk persistence — 2026-10-28

- **Change:** Master UnlockChunkHandler now defers owner release while ChunkLockManager has a preannounced pending chunk write. This closes the Master-side race where a new owner could be granted before the former owner’s save reached durable region storage. No live world was read or changed.
- **Regression:** Added MasterIntegrationTest.chunkOwnershipDoesNotTransferBeforePreannouncedChestSaveIsPersisted: Backend A owns the chunk and announces a delayed save; it requests unlock; Backend B queues for ownership; A then writes a chest snapshot containing 17 items. Assertions require B to remain non-owner before write completion, A’s write to be accepted, ownership to transfer afterward, and B’s forced read to match the exact saved bytes and item count.
- **Result:** Temurin Java 25.0.3+9 / Gradle 9.8 focused run passed **1/1**, zero failures/errors/skips (0.075 s test time). JUnit XML: MultiPaper-Master/build/test-results/test/TEST-puregero.multipaper.server.MasterIntegrationTest.xml (635 bytes, timestamp 2026-10-28 21:17:54Z). Full Master-module tests and post-change reproducible builds are still required; keep status TESTING.
